# Skills · W · part 3

820 entries, wake-review to wallet-analysis. [Index](README.md) · [Everything](../README.md)

- **wake-review**: Reviews a Wake change, branch, pull request or design against the product roadmap, covering phase scope, the six principles, the stack picks and overengineering. Use when asked to review, check or sanity check Wake code or a design; before opening a pull request in the Wake repo; or when someone asks "does this match the roadmap", "is this overengineered" or "did we drift". ([usewake/wake](https://github.com/usewake/wake/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/wake-review) · ★1)
- **wake-scope**: Decides what may be built in the Wake repo right now and what has to wait, based on the current roadmap phase and its gate. Use this before starting any feature, component, integration, refactor or infrastructure work in Wake; whenever someone says "let's add", "should we build", or proposes a new dependency, service or abstraction; when planning a sprint or breaking a phase into tasks; and whenever work seems to reach past the current phase (evidence packs, detection rules, flow policies, rollback, policy compilation, Kafka, a graph database, multi-region, plugin systems). ([usewake/wake](https://github.com/usewake/wake/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/wake-scope) · ★1)
- **wake-sessions**: Schedule a wake-up of other existing Claude Code or Codex sessions after a session or quota limit resets. Discover their exact cmux surfaces, Herdr agents or other supported live addresses, plan delivery now, then use delayed-work:until to wake this caller and nudge the same targets at the requested time. Use for 'wake these sessions at 3pm', 'resume those rate-limited agents after reset', or 'schedule a continuation prompt to these sessions'. ([jtsternberg/claude-plugins](https://github.com/jtsternberg/claude-plugins/tree/HEAD/plugins/delayed-work/skills/wake-sessions) · ★6)

Use when the user wants to: stop an idle EC2 / ECS / Auto Scaling Group / RDS and auto-start it on traffic or on a schedule; add a "serverless-ish" sleep mode to an always-on single box; save cloud spend by shutting down non-prod compute off-hours; hibernate / auto-start-stop / schedule a server.
